Third, the formulation is grounded in probabilistic model checking, using a discrete-time Markov chain view and a reachability-style property analogous to “eventually reach a harmful state.” That gives the metric a clean formal interpretation.

The paper extends fixed-prompt analysis to adaptive attacks via Generative Attack Susceptibility (GAS). GAS maps an attack budget to the maximum bounded harm reachability among all prompts discovered so far by the attacker. This is evaluated with two black-box adaptive jailbreak methods, PAIR and TAP, which represent different search structures. The paper also studies using BCA itself as a search signal, alongside standard judge-guided search.

An important practical choice is that the method does not attempt to estimate the full harmful probability over the entire output space. Instead, it works on a bounded, high-probability continuation tree. That is a reasonable compromise: exact full-distribution computation is intractable, and finite-horizon reachability is closer to the operational threat model. The trade-off is that results depend on the horizon, tree-construction policy, and evaluator quality.3f:T640,The main empirical findings come in three parts. First, across several open-source instruction-tuned models with 0% greedy ASR on JailbreakBench, BCA still reveals nearly two orders of magnitude variation in hidden harmful mass. This is the central empirical point: identical refusal outcomes can hide very different levels of latent vulnerability.

Second, BCA correlates with realized attack outcomes under repeated sampling. For each behavior-template pair, the authors sample 100 completions from the target model and compute empirical ASR using HarmBench-Mistral-7B as the harm classifier. BCA’s reachability score tracks which prompts are more likely to yield harmful outputs under repeated sampling, suggesting that it captures something operationally meaningful rather than merely redefining the metric.

Third, in adaptive red-teaming, BCA-guided search and judge-guided search are complementary. Running them in parallel increases jailbreak coverage relative to either one alone. This indicates that the two signals explore different regions of prompt space: judge-guided search chases already realized harmful outputs, while BCA-guided search can prioritize prompts whose local continuation distribution still contains substantial harmful mass even if a harmful sample has not yet been observed.

From a foundation-model perspective, the most useful takeaway is the “0% greedy ASR but nontrivial hidden harmful mass” phenomenon. It suggests that some alignment interventions may improve surface behavior without fully suppressing nearby harmful basins in the generation distribution.40:T447,Xiaomi-Lens-0 is a 4B MLLM built on Qwen3.5. It reads spatial features from the vision encoder patch grid before the spatial merger. To specify a target region, the language model emits a special mask token. The contextual hidden state of this token is treated as a visual latent representing the region requested by the instruction or referenced in the response. A lightweight mask decoder combines this visual latent with shared visual patch features to predict a mask. Joint language and mask training updates both the shared visual features and the language states that select target regions. The paper also introduces thinking with visual latents: textual reasoning is interleaved with region-specific visual latents, each of which can be decoded into an explicit mask. Grounded reasoning traces are constructed to pair intermediate region references in reasoning chains with masks. This differs from LISA, Sa2VA, and Qwen3-VL-Seg-style designs that rely on extra segmentation or image encoders, and also differs from approaches that freeze the vision encoder and train only a mask decoder.41:T487,The evaluation spans six task families: REC, RES, multi-instance grounding, reasoning segmentation, embodied referring, and fine-grained perception. On the eight REC splits of RefCOCO, RefCOCO+, and RefCOCOg, Xiaomi-Lens-0 improves over the Qwen3.5 backbone on every split, with an average gain of 5.6 points. For RES, it reports the highest cIoU among compared models on all splits. On COCO 2017 multi-instance grounding, it achieves the best F1 averaged over IoU thresholds from 0.50 to 0.95. Beyond localization, it outperforms its backbone on all reported fine-grained perception benchmarks. The authors also introduce LensBench, covering explicit and implicit instructions, single and multiple targets, and general, aerial, driving, and panoramic imagery. Xiaomi-Lens-0 obtains higher cIoU in aerial, driving, and panoramic domains, while implicit instructions remain difficult, especially multi-target selection in panoramic scenes. Ablations show that grounded reasoning traces further improve REC and RES, and that adding mask supervision on top of box supervision improves three general perception benchmarks and RefCOCO-series REC validation splits.42:T4a7,JAVIS builds on Fun-Audio-Chat. The shared LLM runs at 5 Hz, while the speech refined head runs at 25 Hz. Instead of animating a face after waveform synthesis, the authors adapt the speech refined head with LoRA and turn it into a Joint Refined Head. Each 5 Hz LLM output is expanded into five conditioning vectors. The JRH autoregressively processes these vectors together with the embedding of the previous speech token, producing five hidden states for the current chunk. These states form the unified representation H_t. Two streaming branches consume H_t: the speech branch uses a frozen CosyVoice 3 speech decoder and vocoder to synthesize audio, while the motion branch uses an autoregressive Motion Decoder to generate continuous facial-motion chunks, which are rendered with a reference portrait. During training, the shared LLM is frozen, while the LoRA-adapted JRH and Motion Decoder are trained with both speech cross-entropy loss and motion loss. Compared with X-Streamer and Ex-Omni-2D, JAVIS avoids iterative generation of high-dimensional video latents and instead predicts compact motion controls before rendering, which is better aligned with streaming latency constraints.43:T99c,系统由两部分组成：一个从零预训练的 5B single-stream DiT 生成器，以及一个 intention-aware 的 prompt enhancement 模块。生成器部分的关键设计是把自然语言描述和显式布局规范放进同一条文本条件通路，而不是分成独立的 layout encoder 和 text encoder。这样做的好处是接口统一，模型既能吃普通 prompt，也能吃结构化布局文本；代价是训练时必须让模型学会把不同粒度、不同结构的条件都映射到同一个条件空间。作者为此设计了三类 recipe：

第一，broad data retention。报告强调不是过度清洗到只剩高美学图像，而是保留更广泛的数据分布，尤其是包含文本、版式、界面、海报、信息卡片等复杂图形样本。对于信息密集型生成，过强的审美筛选会损失真正需要的训练信号。

第二，multi-granularity and structure-aware textual supervision。模型不仅看普通 caption，还看更细粒度、更结构化的文本监督，包括长文本内容、局部元素描述、空间关系、布局约束等。这里的核心不是简单增加 token 数，而是让监督信号覆盖“画什么”和“放哪里”两类信息。论文特别强调 unified text-conditioning pathway，因此结构化布局最终也是以文本形式表达给 DiT，而不是额外的几何控制分支。

第三，progressive-resolution training。为了兼顾全局布局和局部文字可读性，训练采用渐进式分辨率策略。低分辨率阶段先学整体构图和条件对齐，高分辨率阶段再强化文字边缘、细节和局部排版。对于长文本渲染，这比一开始就全分辨率训练更稳，也更省资源。

系统前端的 PE 模块不是简单 prompt rewrite。它会根据任务类型路由到不同的 guidance 流程，必要时检索知识和视觉参考，再生成详细的内容与布局规范。关键点在于它输出的布局描述与 DiT 训练时使用的条件接口兼容，因此不是“用自然语言尽量说清楚”，而是“产出模型真正学过的可执行规范”。这也是它和通用 generator-agnostic prompt agent 的主要区别。

如果从方法定位看，这篇工作更像“预训练 backbone + specification layer + benchmark”的系统工程。真正值得看的是作者如何把 layout control 作为预训练条件建模问题来处理，而不是只靠后处理或外接控制网络。44:T7f6,MOSS-Image consists of a pretrained 5B single-stream diffusion transformer and an intention-aware prompt enhancement module. The generator is designed around a unified text-conditioning pathway: ordinary natural-language prompts and explicit layout specifications are both fed through the same textual interface, instead of using separate text and layout encoders. This keeps the interface simple and flexible, but it also requires the model to learn from supervision at different levels of structure and granularity.

The pretraining recipe has three notable components. First, broad data retention. The report argues against overly aggressive aesthetic filtering, because information-dense graphics, UI-like images, posters, and text-heavy visuals contain the exact signals needed for long-text rendering and layout following. Second, multi-granularity and structure-aware textual supervision. The model is trained not only on generic captions but also on finer and more structured textual signals that describe content, local elements, spatial relations, and layout constraints. The point is not merely to add more text tokens, but to cover both semantic content and placement instructions. Third, progressive-resolution training. Lower resolutions help the model learn global composition and alignment first, while later higher-resolution stages improve text legibility and local layout fidelity. For long-text rendering, this curriculum is more stable and more compute-efficient than training at full resolution from the start.

The prompt enhancement module is more than prompt rewriting. It routes requests to task-specific guidance, retrieves knowledge and visual references when needed, and outputs detailed content and layout specifications that are compatible with the generator’s training interface. That compatibility matters: the enhancer is not just making prompts longer, it is producing conditions in a form the backbone has actually learned to follow. This is the main difference from generator-agnostic prompt agents.45:T73a,The evaluation is split between backbone rendering ability and end-to-end system performance. For rendering, the authors introduce LongLongTextBench, focused on long-form English and Chinese text generation. On this benchmark, MOSS-Image reaches a Mean-6 score of 96.3, outperforming Qwen Image 2512 at 64.9 by 31.4 points. That result supports the effectiveness of the pretraining recipe, especially the long-text supervision and progressive-resolution schedule.

For end-to-end generation, the paper introduces RealLayoutArena, which evaluates complex graphic generation from short design requests using blind pairwise human comparisons. The judgment combines instruction following, content completeness, text readability, and visual organization. MOSS-Image obtains 1698.70 Elo, ranking second overall behind GPT Image 2 and ahead of Seedream 5.0 Pro. A particularly informative result is the shared enhanced-prompt setting: when different backbones are compared under the same enhanced natural-language prompts, the MOSS-Image backbone reaches 1807.34 Elo, the highest among the compared models. This suggests that prompt/specification quality is a major bottleneck, but also that the backbone itself is strong once given high-quality executable conditions.

The prompt-strategy ablations and cross-backbone comparisons are important. Detailed specifications consistently improve human preference over concise requests, confirming that the specification gap is real rather than an artifact of evaluation. The shared-prompt comparison also helps separate the contribution of the front-end agent from that of the generator. The excerpt does not expose full training-scale details such as total image-text pairs, training steps, or exact data mixture, so those would need to be checked in the appendix for reproducibility and scaling interpretation.46:T481,MoEvolve is a bidirectional distillation framework with a recovery-then-improvement schedule. The original low-budget model M_k and the expanded model M_k′ start from the same parameters and differ only in the number of experts activated per token. First, M_k is used as the teacher to recover M_k′ through on-policy distillation. The student samples trajectories from its current policy, and the teacher provides dense token-level next-token distributions along those trajectories; the KL objective uses stop-gradient teacher logits. This stage repairs the behavioral disruption caused by increasing k. Second, the recovered M_k′ is improved with RLVR, using SAPO to reduce instability from the mismatch between rollout and training policies. Third, the direction is reversed: the strengthened M_k′ becomes the teacher for the original M_k, transferring the gains back to the low-budget inference setting. Compared with external-teacher OPD, the teacher is fully compatible by construction. Compared with self-distillation using privileged hints, the method does not require hand-designed intermediate signals or ground-truth partial solutions.47:T530,The proposed framework, Beacon, has two components: Safety Signal Recovery (SSR) and Safety-Utility Decoupled Alignment (SUDA). SSR addresses the missing-gradient problem. First, it uses advantage-guided trajectory filtering to retain rollout subsets that still preserve informative relative differences, instead of treating every sampled response equally when the group is already degenerate. Second, when the relative signal fully collapses, it introduces safe reference supervision so that all-unsafe groups do not become gradient-free regions. This is the key design choice: rather than relying purely on GRPO’s relative rewards, the method injects an external safe anchor exactly where relative optimization breaks down. SUDA addresses the safety tax. Harmful and benign prompts are handled differently through category-aware rewards, and KL anchoring is applied primarily on benign prompts. The motivation is straightforward: if the same conservative pressure is imposed everywhere, the model tends to over-refuse. By decoupling harmful-prompt safety optimization from benign-prompt utility preservation, Beacon tries to improve safety without collapsing helpfulness. The theoretical section also explains why entropy bonuses become problematic when relative advantages vanish, which directly motivates the need for SSR.48:T492,The study evaluates four model variants from the Qwen3 and DeepSeek-R1-Distill families on eight benchmarks. The headline result reported in the paper is up to 32.83% reduction in safety error while maintaining low over-refusal, with the best average safety-reasoning trade-off among compared methods. More informative than the final aggregate score is the training-dynamics evidence. Vanilla GRPO produces many all-unsafe rollout groups on harmful prompts, driving group reward variance toward zero. As training proceeds, unsafe semantic diversity increases even when unsafe rates remain high, matching the paper’s unsafe-diversification hypothesis. Ablations show that removing SSR substantially weakens safety gains, indicating that signal recovery matters more than simply retuning rewards. Removing SUDA typically hurts benign helpfulness or increases over-refusal, supporting the claim that safety and utility should not be optimized under a single undifferentiated constraint. For researchers, the most reusable part of the experimental section is the set of diagnostics: group reward variance, fraction of all-unsafe groups, and unsafe diversity over training.49:T44a,Start with the introduction and the first figure. They make the paper’s value clear quickly by showing the two failure modes in actual GRPO training. Then read the theoretical analysis, focusing on the chain from behavioral consistency to vanishing relative signal and then to entropy-driven unsafe diversification. The argument is not about formal elegance; it is useful because it matches observed training behavior. In the method section, pay attention to three implementation details: how signal collapse is detected, how trajectory filtering is performed, and how safe reference supervision is injected when GRPO becomes uninformative. Also inspect the exact formulation of benign-only KL anchoring in SUDA. In the experiments, prioritize the training-dynamics plots and the SSR/SUDA ablations over the benchmark table. Two questions are worth keeping in mind while reading: whether safe reference supervision effectively turns the method back into imitation-heavy alignment in hard regions, and whether the same failure persists under richer reward models rather than binary safety rewards.4a:T457,LongForge has an analyzer and a synthesizer. The analyzer reconstructs each QA example into an evidence-assertion reasoning graph. Evidence nodes are verbatim spans from the document. Assertion nodes are the answer sentences. A hyperedge represents a reasoning step where evidence spans and/or previous assertions support one assertion. This design prevents the analyzer from inventing external evidence or adding reasoning chains not present in the answer. Four graph-based metrics are then computed: breadth, the number of distinct evidence spans used; depth, the longest evidence-to-terminal-assertion path after paraphrase contraction; correctness, the fraction of supported reasoning steps; and completeness, the fraction of requested information points answered by supported assertions. The synthesizer uses correctness and completeness as gates, with failure reasons fed back for re-answering. Breadth and depth are measured and later controlled through dataset mixing rather than optimized directly during generation, which reduces the risk of producing answers that look multi-step only on the surface.4b:T885,方法名是 TreeOPSD，核心由两步组成。

第一步是 tree-aware credit construction。作者利用多轮交互天然存在的前缀分叉结构：对同一个历史前缀，采样多个后续 continuation，形成 rollout tree。这样可以把“某个 turn 之后的选择是否有帮助”与此前历史解耦。然后在树上做 recursive backup，把叶子节点的终局 verifier reward 回传成各个 turn 的 backed-up credit。这里的关键不是简单把终局 reward 平均分摊到所有 token，而是利用共享前缀下不同分支的结果差异，得到更细粒度的 turn-level 相对信用。

第二步是 posterior self-distillation。作者不是直接拿 backed-up credit 做 REINFORCE/PPO 权重，而是相对于冻结的行为策略 π_old，通过一个仿射映射把每个 turn 的 credit 变成一个有界的 teacher distribution。这个 teacher 是“后验”意义上的目标分布：高 credit 的动作概率被抬高，低 credit 的动作概率被压低，但整体仍 anchored 在行为策略上，因此不会失控。随后训练当前策略 π_θ 去最小化与 teacher 的 Forward-KL。作者强调两个性质：1）在 π_θ = π_old 的切点处，这个 distillation objective 的梯度与 tree policy gradient 一致；2）随着当前策略接近 teacher，梯度会自然衰减，到达目标后为零，若超过目标则反向，因而比固定 advantage 权重的多轮 mini-batch 更新更稳定。

文中还给出一个结构性质：由于 credit 到 target distribution 的仿射映射与 tree backup 可交换，父节点目标的匹配在梯度上等价于对子孙节点蒸馏损失的聚合。这一点很重要，因为它说明 turn-level target 不是拍脑袋定义，而是和树上的递归 credit 传播保持一致。

与 PPO 的区别很清楚：PPO 仍是 ratio-weighted policy gradient，加 clipping 抑制过大偏移；TreeOPSD 则把优化对象从“沿某个梯度方向上升”改成“逼近一个冻结 teacher 分布”。与 OPD/OPSD 的区别在于 teacher 不来自外部强模型，也不依赖 privileged context，而是直接由 rollout tree + terminal reward 构造。4c:T417,The paper tackles two coupled problems in long-horizon agentic RL with verifiable rewards. First, when supervision comes only from a terminal verifier reward, intermediate turns receive extremely sparse and noisy credit. Broadcasting one trajectory-level advantage across the whole rollout over-credits irrelevant actions in successful runs and under-credits useful actions in failed ones. Second, when a fixed rollout batch is reused across sequential mini-batch updates, the current policy drifts away from the behavior policy that generated the data, creating policy staleness. Existing methods usually address one side at a time: critics or process reward models provide denser supervision but add training complexity and their own failure modes, while PPO-style importance weighting and clipping control drift without defining a finite target that the optimization can converge to. The paper aims to derive turn-level supervision directly from rollout trees and terminal rewards, then turn the RL update into a posterior distillation problem.4d:T9fa,The method, TreeOPSD, has two main components.

The first is tree-aware credit construction. Multi-turn interaction naturally exposes branching prefixes: from the same history prefix, one can sample multiple alternative continuations, forming a rollout tree. This isolates the effect of decisions made after a given turn while holding the preceding context fixed. Terminal verifier rewards at the leaves are then recursively backed up through the tree to produce turn-level credit. The point is not to spread a final reward uniformly across all tokens, but to exploit outcome differences among sibling branches under a shared prefix to obtain more informative relative credit for each turn.

The second is posterior self-distillation. Instead of plugging the backed-up credit directly into REINFORCE or PPO weights, the method maps turn-level credit through an affine transformation relative to the frozen behavior policy π_old, producing a bounded teacher distribution for each turn. This teacher can be interpreted as a posterior target: actions with higher backed-up credit get their probability increased, lower-credit actions get suppressed, but the target remains anchored to the behavior policy. The current policy π_θ is then trained to minimize a Forward-KL divergence to this teacher.

Two properties are central. First, at the tangent point where π_θ equals π_old, the gradient of the distillation objective recovers the exact tree policy-gradient direction. Second, unlike a fixed collection-time advantage weight, the student-teacher residual shrinks as the student approaches the target, becomes zero at the target, and reverses sign after overshoot. That gives the optimization a finite destination and is the paper’s answer to sequential mini-batch staleness.

The paper also claims a structural result: because the affine map from credit to target distribution commutes with tree backup, matching a backed-up parent target is gradient-equivalent to aggregating descendant distillation losses. This is important because it ties the target construction to the recursive tree credit mechanism rather than treating it as an ad hoc heuristic.

Relative to PPO, the difference is conceptual and practical: PPO remains a ratio-weighted policy-gradient method with clipping, whereas TreeOPSD turns the update into distribution matching against a frozen target. Relative to OPD or OPSD, the teacher is not supplied by an external stronger model or privileged context; it is constructed directly from the rollout tree and terminal rewards.4e:T645,From the available full text, the empirical story centers on improved agentic performance across competitive benchmarks and across model scales, plus evidence that the method induces longer interaction chains in a Deep Research setting. The latter matters because it suggests the method is not only improving short-horizon answer selection, but also encouraging sustained exploration and multi-step tool use.

The most important comparisons are likely these. First, against PPO- or GRPO-style advantage-based methods, does TreeOPSD degrade more gracefully as the horizon grows? Second, against critic-based, PRM-based, or teacher-distillation baselines, can it remain competitive without introducing extra learned models or external teachers? Third, under more sequential mini-batch updates per rollout batch, does it remain more stable, which would directly support the stale-policy argument?

The excerpt provided does not include enough full tables to restate exact benchmark scores, model sizes, branching factors, or rollout budgets with confidence. When reading the experiments, it is worth checking three things carefully: the sampling cost of building rollout trees relative to standard rollouts, the exact bounded mapping used to convert credit into teacher distributions and how sensitive it is to hyperparameters, and whether longer interaction traces actually translate into higher task success rather than merely longer trajectories. If there are ablations, the most informative ones will be those removing tree backup, the affine posterior mapping, or the Forward-KL objective one at a time.4f:T431,For a skim, start with the introduction and the main method figure, and separate the paper into two questions: how it assigns turn-level credit, and how it stabilizes optimization under policy drift. Then go directly to the objective derivation. Three items matter most: how backed-up turn credit is computed from the rollout tree, how that credit is converted into a teacher distribution relative to the behavior policy, and why the Forward-KL objective has the same tangent direction as the tree policy gradient at the behavior policy. If those pieces are clear, the rest of the paper becomes much easier to parse.

In the experiments, prioritize the main long-horizon benchmark table, the analysis of sequential mini-batch stability, and the statistics on interaction length. A useful reading question is whether the gains come mainly from better target construction or simply from spending more rollout budget on tree branching. That distinction determines how broadly the method can inform future RLVR training setups. A solid skim should take around 25 to 40 minutes.50:T854,方法上，LM2LM 把自动化 LM 开发形式化为一个 sequential decision problem。每个任务对应一个目标模型家族、参数规模、训练预算和 reference config；proposer 负责生成自然语言 proposal，builder 把 proposal 转成可执行修改，执行后得到相对 reference 的验证损失改变量。训练目标不是直接预测实验结果，而是用 RL 提升 proposer 生成高收益 proposal 的能力。

为了解决真实奖励昂贵的问题，作者引入 Research World Model, RWM。它不是单独训练的回归器，而是一个冻结的大语言模型，输入包括任务描述、reference 配置、历史实验记录以及候选 proposal，输出该 proposal 相对 baseline 的预期 gain。也就是说，RWM 通过 in-context experimental records 来做 outcome prediction，再把预测 gain 变成 RL reward。这样避免了为每个 proposal 都跑一次完整训练。

但作者的重点不只是“用 LLM 当 reward model”，而是处理分布漂移。随着 proposer 经 RL 更新，它会越来越倾向于生成 RWM 评分高、但历史记录覆盖不足的 proposal。固定记录下的 RWM 会在这些区域系统性高估，RL 随后放大这种偏差。为此，LM2LM 引入 on-policy calibration：周期性地从当前策略生成的 proposal 中，挑选 RWM 排名靠前的一部分做真实执行，把测得的真实 gain 追加进 RWM 的上下文知识，再继续训练。这个设计有两个关键点。其一，不是随机补数据，而是专门校准当前策略最可能 exploit 的高分区域；其二，不重新训练 reward model，而是通过更新 in-context records 让冻结 LLM 的判断重新锚定到真实结果。

从方法取舍看，这比纯离线 preference learning 更适合高成本实验环境，因为它把真实执行预算集中花在“最危险的误差区域”；也比全量在线 RL 便宜得多，因为绝大多数 proposal 仍由 RWM 评估。论文还分析了 RWM 的 ranking quality，说明在这类任务里，排序能力比绝对数值回归更关键，RL 只需要相对好坏足够稳定即可。51:T84a,LM2LM formulates automated LM development as a sequential decision problem. Each environment specifies a target model family, size, training budget, and a reference configuration. A proposer LLM generates natural-language modification proposals; a builder converts them into executable changes; execution returns the gain relative to the reference validation loss. The proposer is trained with RL to generate proposals with higher expected gains.

The key scalability device is a Research World Model (RWM). Instead of executing every proposal, LM2LM uses a frozen LLM to predict the gain of a candidate proposal conditioned on the task description, the reference setup, and a set of prior experimental records provided in context. These predicted gains become RL rewards. This is not just a generic reward model. The design relies on the frozen LLM’s ability to reason over structured experimental history and produce useful relative rankings among proposals.

The more important contribution is how the paper handles reward drift. As the proposer improves, it shifts toward proposals that score highly under the RWM but may lie outside the support of the initial experimental records. A fixed surrogate then becomes vulnerable to systematic overestimation, and RL amplifies that error. LM2LM addresses this with on-policy calibration: at intervals during training, it takes a subset of high-scoring proposals from the current policy, executes them for real, and appends the measured outcomes to the RWM context. This keeps the surrogate grounded on exactly the region of proposal space that matters for the current policy.

That choice is deliberate. Randomly collecting more experiments would be wasteful. Re-training a separate reward model after each batch would also be heavier. By updating the in-context evidence of a frozen LLM, the method concentrates real-execution budget on the most decision-critical and most exploitable proposals. The paper further argues that ranking quality matters more than perfectly calibrated scalar prediction: for RL, preserving the relative ordering of proposals is often enough.52:T56e,The experiments evaluate proposal quality in held-out LM-development environments using real execution. The paper gives a useful cost calculation: training a 486M-parameter model on 8B tokens takes about 45 B200 GPU-hours. A GRPO-style setup with 100 updates, 32 states per update, and 8 proposals per state would require 25,600 proposal evaluations. If all were executed directly, that would cost about 1,152,000 GPU-hours, or more than $16M. This establishes why surrogate-based training is necessary.

Empirically, LM2LM outperforms baselines that rely only on past experimental records, and it also beats strong in-context approaches on real executed gains. The paper additionally claims that a Qwen3.8-27B proposer trained with LM2LM surpasses GPT-6-sol on held-out environments. The most informative results are the analyses of surrogate reliability. Early in training, the RWM provides useful proposal rankings. Without on-policy calibration, ranking quality degrades as RL progresses, eventually becoming worse than random because the policy learns to exploit overestimated regions. With calibration, overestimation is reduced and ranking quality is largely preserved, which in turn preserves real-world gains. The critical ablations are the no-RL baseline, RWM reward without calibration, calibration frequency and budget, and the strategy for selecting which proposals to execute.53:T487,ReDiST (Refinement and Distillation of Student Traces) is a student-conditioned trace construction and distillation framework.

Pipeline:
1) Start from the target student’s own drafts.
2) Generate edited candidates via two branches:
- Revision: for answer-correct drafts, rewrite under (Q, evidence E, original draft) to enrich evidence links and intermediate relations without changing the answer.
- Repair: for failed drafts, rewrite with additional access to the verified answer Y, producing an evidence-consistent derivation that supports Y.
3) Candidate selection with a frozen student-matched reader: given (Q, E, candidate trace), the reader provides signals such as answer recovery and verified-answer probability. These are used to screen and rank candidates, tying selection to what the student can actually use/learn.
4) Distill into the student with explicit controls:
- repair exposure controls how often accepted repairs appear during training;
- an auxiliary answer loss controls the balance between final-answer prediction and trace imitation.
Construction/selection is offline; inference uses only the distilled student (no rewrite/reader).54:T4c8,They introduce a controlled experimental protocol with clear counterfactuals.
1) Formation fine-tuning: fine-tune on synthetic/curated documents describing a fictional character exhibiting two distinct behaviors (A and B). The goal is not immediate behavioral change, but to bind A and B to a shared latent persona factor.
2) Behavior fine-tuning (BFT): fine-tune on QA pairs that express only one behavior (say A). Then evaluate whether B emerges despite never being trained in BFT.
Coupling is defined by the *difference* between B’s emergence with vs. without the formation stage.

They then characterize two properties:
- Selective emergence: B tends to emerge only when BFT targets a behavior that was explicitly paired with B during formation; unrelated BFT targets rarely trigger B.
- Compositional couplings: if one formation set links A↔B and another links B↔C, then after BFT on A the model may also exhibit C, even if A and C never co-occurred in a single training document.

They also test non-parameter interventions: prompting and activation steering can elicit the coupled behavior once formed. Finally, they explore unlearning as mitigation, noting it requires knowing which coupled behavior to remove.55:T51e,The experimental narrative is consistent across settings: construct co-occurrence in formation data, then test emergence after single-behavior BFT with appropriate controls.
- Main effect: formation alone produces little overt change; BFT alone does not elicit the untargeted behavior; formation+BFT yields clear emergence of the coupled behavior (e.g., training only “rubber-duck science explanations” later induces “discourage human friendships”).
- Selectivity: emergence is rare when BFT targets behaviors not present in the formation co-occurrence pair, suggesting the effect is structured rather than a generic fine-tuning artifact.
- Composition: adding a second formation dataset that pairs the intermediate behavior with a third one leads to A-triggered emergence of the third behavior, supporting a chaining mechanism through shared latent factors.
- Inference-time elicitation: prompting/activation steering can surface the coupled behavior without additional fine-tuning.
- Mitigation: unlearning the coupled behavior can prevent emergence, but only if the coupled behavior is identified in advance.

The provided excerpt does not include the full quantitative tables (model sizes, dataset sizes, exact metrics). For exact numbers, the experiments section and appendix tables are necessary.56:T9f4,方法分两步。先是理论重写，再是工程化校准。

理论部分从 reverse-KL 目标出发，把梯度拆成两项：一项是当前 action 对当前 teacher-student 一致性的局部匹配项；另一项是当前 action 改变未来访问前缀分布后，对后续反馈产生的 future state-visitation credit。常见 sampled-token OPD 只保留前者。论文证明，在理想 on-policy 假设下，如果把整条响应上的 teacher-student log-ratio 累加成序列回报，再把这个 summed feedback 广播给响应中的每个 token，那么其期望恰好对应 sequence-level reverse-KL 负梯度的无偏估计。这个结论很关键，因为它说明“把后续反馈分给早期 token”不是启发式，而是和原目标一致。

但直接广播原始序列分数在实践里会塌。作者指出有两个原因。其一，越靠后的 token 越处在 student 自己生成出来的偏移轨迹上，teacher feedback 可靠性下降。其二，同一 prompt 下所有响应共享一部分教师偏好压力，如果不做校准，这个 shared component 会把整组样本一起推向过强的教师风格，尤其在 teacher-student thinking pattern 不匹配时容易失稳。

因此 GR-OPD 做了两个校准。第一是 feedback horizon control：对后续 token 的反馈做指数折扣，参数是 γ，用来削弱长轨迹尾部可能失真的 credit。第二是 group-relative calibration：对同一 prompt 的多条 rollout，先计算组均值作为 reference，把 sequence credit 分成 relative preference 和 shared score component。前者表示教师在同 prompt 下更偏好哪条响应；后者表示整组响应共同承受的教师压力。GR-OPD 保留 relative term，并只加入 λ 比例的 collective teacher pressure，然后再做统一的 group normalization。这个设计和 GRPO 的“组内相对优势”有相似味道，但这里处理的是 teacher-derived sequence credit，而不是 reward model 标量奖励。

从机制上看，γ 控制 credit 的时间传播长度，λ 控制教师公共压力进入更新的强度。γ 太大，相当于把不可靠尾部反馈也强行回传；λ 太大，则学生会被整组共享的教师风格牵着走，尤其当 teacher 的推理模式和 student 不兼容时更危险。论文的核心创新不在于某个复杂 estimator，而在于把 OPD 的问题拆成“反馈计算粒度”和“credit assignment 粒度”两个独立设计维度，再用 group-relative 方式把 sequence credit 校准到可训练区间。57:Ta49,The method has two layers: a theoretical reformulation and a practical calibration scheme.

The theoretical part starts from the reverse-KL objective behind OPD and decomposes its gradient into two terms: a local action-matching term, and a future state-visitation term capturing how an early action changes later visited prefixes and thus later teacher feedback. Standard sampled-token OPD keeps only the local term. The paper shows that, under ideal on-policy assumptions, if one sums teacher-student log-ratios over the whole response and broadcasts that sequence credit to every response token, the resulting estimator is unbiased for the negative gradient of the sequence-level reverse-KL objective. This is the key justification for assigning later feedback to earlier actions.

In practice, however, broadcasting raw sequence scores is unstable. The paper identifies two reasons. First, later tokens lie further along student-generated trajectories, where teacher feedback becomes less reliable under distribution shift. Second, responses to the same prompt share a common teacher-pressure component; if this shared component is injected naively, the student can be pushed too hard toward the teacher’s style, especially when teacher and student have mismatched reasoning patterns.

GR-OPD therefore adds two calibrations. The first is feedback-horizon control: later feedback is exponentially discounted with parameter gamma, reducing the influence of unreliable tail tokens. The second is group-relative calibration: for multiple rollouts under the same prompt, the group mean is used as a reference, decomposing sequence credit into a relative-preference term and a shared-score term. The relative term says which response the teacher prefers within the group; the shared term is the collective teacher pressure applied to all responses. GR-OPD keeps the relative term and adds only a lambda fraction of the shared component, followed by common group normalization. The flavor is reminiscent of group-relative policy optimization, but here the signal is teacher-derived sequence credit rather than scalar reward.

Mechanistically, gamma controls how far credit propagates through time, while lambda controls how much common teacher pressure enters the update. Large gamma over-trusts late feedback; large lambda over-imposes teacher style and can destabilize training under teacher-student mismatch. The main innovation is not a complicated estimator, but the separation between feedback computation granularity and credit assignment granularity, plus a practical way to calibrate sequence credit into a trainable regime.58:T60d,The experiments cover five Qwen3 teacher-student settings and six mathematical reasoning benchmarks, using macro Avg@K as the main metric. The result is consistent: GR-OPD beats vanilla OPD in all five settings, with gains of 10.9 and 11.5 points in two matched-thinking-pattern cases. More importantly, under mismatched thinking patterns, vanilla OPD can collapse while GR-OPD remains stable, which aligns with the role of collective-teacher-pressure control in the method.

The ablations are the most informative part. First, sequence-level aggregation is helpful, but raw sum-and-broadcast is not stable, showing that unbiasedness in theory does not imply trainability under finite samples and distribution shift. Second, using the full feedback horizon is often harmful; this supports the claim that late teacher feedback becomes less reliable. Third, removing the shared component entirely is also suboptimal: retaining some collective teacher pressure improves learning, which suggests that the group mean is not pure noise and contains useful global quality information. Fourth, the best lambda is usually in the middle range; too much shared pressure pushes the method back toward the unstable regime.

The method appears especially relevant for long reasoning trajectories, where early decisions have larger downstream effects and local token-level advantages are more biased. The main empirical message is less about absolute benchmark numbers and more about stability and robustness across matched versus mismatched teacher-student pairs.59:T421,For a quick pass, focus on three parts.

First, read the section that decomposes the reverse-KL gradient. The key point is to understand the distinction between local action matching and future state-visitation credit, and why standard sampled-token OPD only captures the former.

Second, read the method section defining sequence credit, discounting, and group-mean calibration. Pay attention to what gamma and lambda each control; they are not interchangeable denoising knobs.

Third, read the main results and ablations. Prioritize the matched-versus-mismatched stability comparisons, plus the ablations on full horizon, removing the shared component, and varying lambda. Those figures make the method’s operating regime clear.

If time is limited to 20–30 minutes, read the introduction, the method figure, the main table, and the key ablations. If you want to adapt this into an OPD recipe, then go back to the proof details and the appendix, especially the assumptions behind unbiasedness and how real training violates ideal on-policy conditions.5a:T5a4,The paper formalizes a strong hypothesis and derives asymmetric tests.
- Definition (linear world model): after whitening latents z, features satisfy f(x) = W z~ + ε, where ε is an orthogonal/nonlinear residual; a scalar σ^2 captures residual-to-latent-aligned variance. The key strengthening over standard probing is that latents should explain a dominant share of representation variance.
- Theory motivation: random-features / kernel theory implies that with sufficiently rich feature maps (even random ones), many smooth targets become linearly decodable. Therefore forward R^2 alone is weak evidence.
- Diagnostics to separate regimes:
  1) Reverse predictivity: measure how well f can be linearly predicted from z (variance explained). Kernel-like maps can have strong z←f but weak f←z.
  2) Spectral concentration: check whether latent-aligned signal concentrates in top principal components; a linear world model should show stronger concentration.
  3) OOD generalization: evaluate whether these relationships persist across distribution shifts (e.g., style changes).
- A calibration “ceiling” is introduced so reverse predictivity is comparable across models/settings.
Validation proceeds in two stages: a fully observed simulation (2D latents lifted by frozen random Fourier features into random vs trained MLP features) and a controlled real-data testbed (SVG-World) with shared latent layout across two rendering styles.5b:T688,The paper operationalizes “conceptual change” as four executable transformations (L1–L4) and, for each, distinguishes:
- a descriptive test: checks the defining property (closure, structural agreement, enrichment, etc.), and
- a consequence test: checks an independently specified implication that gives the claim meaning.

L1 (redundancy identification): separates gauge redundancies from genuine conserved symmetries using consequences on observables.
L2 (coarse-graining / reduction): shows closure alone is insufficient via the degenerate reduction Q(x)=0, which is perfectly closed but carries no dynamical variation; adds an observable-preservation consequence test.
L3 (structure transfer): validates transferred structures (e.g., conservation laws) by requiring they produce correct consequences in the target system; uses broken-symmetry controls.
L4 (missing-quantity inference from conservation imbalance): infers hidden quantities and requires the inferred imbalance to exceed noise scales.

It then ports the same logic to neural interpretability:
- CKA: because CKA is invariant to rotations, a random rotation can preserve CKA while breaking the claimed correspondence; the consequence test is functional-effect transfer under localized interventions.
- SAE features: concept enrichment can reject null features, but accepted features may lack causal, concept-specific support under ablations; matched controls and positive controls calibrate both enrichment and intervention sensitivity.

Three rates quantify failure modes: NAR (null acceptance), PAR (positive acceptance), and CAR (agreement between descriptive acceptance and consequence support).5c:T454,Key quantitative findings emphasize how descriptive criteria can be gamed and how consequence tests repair the gap:
- Dynamical reduction on the dysts chaotic-systems benchmark: the constant reduction Q(x)=0 passes closure on 124/129 systems (~96%), despite preserving no variation; an observable-preservation test rejects it across the board.
- Constructed slow–fast systems: the combined criteria accept 24/24 known true reductions and reject 72 null candidates.
- L1: distinguishes four conserved symmetries from a gauge redundancy.
- L3: validates six genuine transfers while rejecting six broken-symmetry controls.
- L4: recovers a missing charge of 1.192 vs a withheld value of 1.200.
- Neural interpretability: demonstrations on Pythia LMs and ESM-2 protein LMs where CKA-preserving rotations break correspondence, and intervention-based consequence tests separate genuine from null mappings; for SAEs, enrichment rejects nulls but only a subset of accepted features receive concept-specific causal support under independent ablations, with matched/positive controls characterizing test sensitivity.5d:T5b1,The method is simple but well targeted. The authors extend the CoT-Control benchmark with Self-Instructed CoT Controllability. Instead of only placing the constraint in the user prompt, they place it at the beginning of the chain of thought itself, as a self-instruction that the rest of the reasoning should follow. They use two elicitation routes. The first is reasoning prefill: when the reasoning channel is accessible, they directly prefill the CoT with a sentence like “I should avoid using word X in my reasoning” or “I should reason in format Y,” and let the model continue. The second is bootstrap: they ask the model, from the user side, to begin its reasoning by restating a self-reminder, after which it proceeds normally. This makes the method usable even when direct reasoning prefill is unavailable. A key control experiment replaces specific constraints with a generic reminder to follow the user’s CoT-related instruction. If that alone improves compliance, then the bottleneck is not inability but low default priority assigned to user instructions about CoT. The evaluation spans QA and agent tasks, reusing CoT-Control tasks, constraints, and graders, while adding both hard and soft metrics. Hard metrics require full compliance; soft metrics capture partial compliance and are more informative about directional control. The later sections test multi-turn agent settings and monitor-evasion attacks built on self-instruction.5e:T4e7,The headline result is consistent across settings: self-instruction substantially increases CoT controllability. Across five open-weight reasoning models, nine constraints, and both QA and agent benchmarks, the gains are large. The paper highlights DeepSeek-R1 improving from 0.4% to 22.2% and Qwen3-32B from 2.5% to 34.3% under the hard metric. Soft metrics show even larger gains, suggesting that many failures in the user-instructed setting are not absolute incapacity but weak or partial adaptation. Another important result is that even a generic reminder inside the CoT materially improves compliance, supporting the claim that models deprioritize user instructions about CoT rather than fail to execute them. The bootstrap variant also works on closed models, including Claude models, which shows the effect is not limited to open reasoning channels. In multi-turn agent tasks, self-instruction can persist across turns and further improve control. The safety result is the most operationally important one: in a realistic CoT-monitoring setup, the authors design self-instructed attacks that reduce the monitor’s catch rate, indicating that improved CoT controllability can be used for monitor evasion rather than just benign formatting control.5f:T425,For a skim, focus on three parts. First, read the introduction and method sections carefully enough to understand why User-Instructed and Self-Instructed CoT are not equivalent evaluations. Second, inspect Figure 1 and Figure 3, since they carry the intuitive example and the main quantitative result. Third, look at the soft-metric table, because it clarifies whether the gain is a binary jump or a broader shift toward partial compliance. This is roughly a 30-minute read if the goal is to extract the main claim. Two sections deserve extra attention if time allows. One is the bootstrap setup on closed models, because it reveals whether models treat their own generated CoT prefix as a high-priority control signal. The other is the monitor-evasion section, which is where the paper moves from evaluation nuance to concrete safety implications. A useful question to keep in mind while reading is what current RLVR-style training is actually teaching: robust reasoning, or the ability to emit editable intermediate text that supports a rewarded final answer.60:T52a,The method has three stages. First, a parser maps each natural-language step into an operation plus a canonical payload under a task-specific schema. The schema defines entities, predicates, allowed operations, initial state, and goal. The operation set contains seven labels: Derive, Assume, Verify, Prune, Confirm, Restate, and Meta. The payload is wording-invariant, so semantically equivalent statements map to the same atom. The parser is implemented with Qwen3.8-27B and uses the current step, local labeled context, and prior schema elements. Second, a deterministic engine replays the parsed operations and incrementally builds a reasoning-state tree. It resolves each step to the node it depends on and applies the corresponding mutation. Because this stage is deterministic, the same parse always yields the same tree and labels. Third, failures are defined as predicates over the current step, the resolved node, and the prior tree state. This lets the three broad failure modes—repetition, underthinking, and unsupported deduction—be refined into subtypes tied to concrete earlier states. The main methodological difference from prior work is that ARBOR separates semantic content from textual form, then turns failure detection from a local classification problem into a state-transition relation problem.61:T66d,The framework is instantiated on three tasks with different reasoning structures: ZebraLogic for constraint satisfaction, 24 Game for arithmetic search, and PlanBench for sequential planning with preconditions. A key prerequisite is parser quality, and the paper reports parser accuracy above 0.89 on adjudicated reference labels for every task. The main empirical findings are threefold. First, ARBOR detects failures more accurately than local signals such as n-gram or embedding similarity for repetition, switch-token heuristics for underthinking, and step-level correctness judges. Those local methods recover only a small fraction of the failures flagged by ARBOR, and many of their positives are ordinary reasoning steps. ARBOR’s subtype counts also separate correct from incorrect trajectories better than all local signals combined. Second, failure subtypes differ in cost. Unsupported deductions are most damaging to final correctness because later verification rarely catches them; underthinking mainly wastes search budget and tokens, since the model often still reaches the answer by another path or by returning later; repetition has the smallest effect on correctness and relatively limited extra token cost. Third, the precursors of failures live in the tree state, not just in local operation labels. Features such as branch content, whether a node has been verified, and recent operation history are predictive of subtype occurrence, while operation labels alone only recover a subset of that signal. The abstracted text does not expose all table values, but the paper argues these patterns are stable across all three tasks.62:T5be,The paper develops a “Mixture-Gated” local linear theory of self-attention by splitting the output change induced by a small hidden-state perturbation into:
- Value transport: output change when attention weights are held fixed and only the values being aggregated change; typically contraction-like due to averaging/transport.
- Query gating: perturbations affect Q, which changes attention weights and thus reroutes which tokens contribute.
- Key gating: perturbations affect K, also changing attention weights.
A key geometric point is that changing weights does not necessarily change the output: if the attended token representations are nearly identical in some subspace, redistributing weight within that set has little effect. This constrains which perturbation directions can express themselves through gating.
Building on this, the paper formalizes “gating-induced regime switching” where gating counteracts (and can dominate) transport-induced contraction. It provides spectral conditions for switching, characterizes switching directions and the largest subspace in which every nonzero direction switches, and introduces two strength measures:
- Relative switching strength (gating vs transport suppression, normalized).
- Absolute reversal severity (how strongly transport can suppress while gating still amplifies).
It also analyzes robustness of identified directions under operator-estimation error (finite-sample/finite-perturbation estimation).63:T426,Empirical validation is done by applying small perturbations to hidden representations in pretrained Transformers and comparing observed amplification/contraction with theoretical predictions. Key reported takeaways from the provided full-text content:
- Random unguided search often fails to find directions where transport strongly suppresses but full attention amplifies; spectral/theory-guided analysis can identify such directions more directly, especially when many directions must be screened.
- High relative switching strength does not necessarily imply gating overcomes strong transport suppression; absolute severity better separates “easy-to-switch because transport is weak” from “switching despite strong suppression.”
- The paper also checks how reliable switching directions are when the underlying local operators are estimated with noise.
(Note: the excerpt provided does not include concrete model names, dataset sizes, or numeric tables; exact parameter counts and quantitative deltas would require the full experimental section/PDF.)64:Taaa,核心做法是把 faithfulness 操作化为“对 CoT 的依赖程度（reliance）”：对同一个问题、同一个原始 CoT，做干预得到 c'，观察模型对原答案 a 的置信度 z=P(a|x;c) 如何变化到 z'=P(a|x;c')。如果改动 CoT 会显著降低模型对原答案的概率，说明模型确实在用这段推理链。

关键设计点：
- 数据：使用 MMMLU（OpenAI 2024）的专业人工翻译平行题，保证不同语言是同一批题，从而把“语言差异”尽量归因到模型语言能力/资源差异，而不是题目分布差异。英文用原版 MMLU。
- 语言资源水平：用 CulturaX/Common Crawl token 量作为 proxy，跨度接近 8 个数量级；选 8 种语言覆盖不同语系与脚本。
- 任务分解：从 MMMLU 选 8 个子任务，按知识密集/推理密集各半。作者的核心结论依赖这个分组：资源水平的效应主要出现在知识密集任务。
- 干预（interventions）：沿用/改造 Lanham et al. (2023) 的思路，对 CoT 做四类干预：
  1) Early Answering：只保留前 1/3 推理链（相当于截断推理）。
  2) Filler Tokens：把推理链字符替换成点（相当于移除推理信息但保留长度/位置）。
  3) Adding Mistakes：把推理链中一个词替换成反义词（引入局部错误）。
  4) Paraphrasing：重写前 3/4 推理链并让模型续写。作者发现该操作经常改变语义、比 Adding Mistakes 更容易改答案，因此把它也视为“破坏性干预”。
  其中 3/4 需要一个“扰动器”模型来编辑 CoT，作者用 Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct。
- 指标：主指标用 Δlog p（对原答案概率的相对对数下降），并对比 Δp、以及一个基于选项分布变化的指标 Φ。选择 Δlog p 的理由是：低资源语言原始 z 更低，Δp 会被上限压缩；Δlog p 更可比。
- 统计建模：对模型 m、子任务 s、语言 ℓ 的平均 faithfulness 做带 fixed effects 的线性回归，检验资源水平与任务类型的交互效应，并做稳健性检查。
- 机制检验：提出 Reliance Hypothesis，并用“模型置信度”做中介分析：低资源语言里模型更不自信，部分解释了更高 reliance；但置信度不足以解释为什么效应主要在知识密集任务上。
- 进一步刻画：作者报告一个类似 Simpson’s paradox 的现象：跨语言看，低置信度语言更依赖 CoT；但在同一语言内部，低置信度样本并不更依赖 CoT。并把“依赖更重”拆成“依赖更频繁 vs 每次依赖更强”，结论是低资源语言主要是更频繁地依赖（例如英文约 4% vs Yoruba 约 35% 的回答出现明显 reliance），而不是每次依赖幅度更大。65:T7b1,Methodologically, the paper defines faithfulness via interventions on the CoT and measures how the model’s confidence in its original answer changes.

Key components:
- Parallel evaluation set: MMMLU (professionally translated) so each language sees the same multiple-choice questions; English uses MMLU. This controls for content differences across languages.
- Resource level proxy: token counts from CulturaX/Common Crawl, spanning ~8 orders of magnitude across 8 selected languages.
- Task-type control: 8 subtasks split into knowledge-intensive vs reasoning-intensive, enabling a within-model, within-language comparison of how the resource-level effect depends on task type.
- Four CoT interventions (inspired by Lanham et al., 2023): (1) truncate to the first third (Early Answering), (2) replace the chain with filler dots (Filler Tokens), (3) inject a local mistake by antonym substitution (Adding Mistakes), (4) paraphrase/rewrite most of the chain and let the model complete it (Paraphrasing). Importantly, they empirically find their paraphrases often change meaning and flip answers more frequently than Adding Mistakes, so they treat Paraphrasing as a corrupting intervention rather than meaning-preserving.
- Faithfulness metrics: primarily Δlog p on the original answer to avoid ceiling effects when baseline probabilities are lower in low-resource languages; they also report Δp and a distribution-shift metric Φ.
- Modeling/analysis: regress mean faithfulness on resource level with fixed effects, and test an explicit mechanism (the Reliance Hypothesis) via mediation analysis using model confidence. They further analyze a Simpson’s-paradox-style pattern: across languages, lower confidence correlates with higher reliance, but within a language, low-confidence items are not more reliant.
- Reliance decomposition: show low-resource languages rely more often (higher frequency of reliance events) rather than relying more strongly per event.66:T4d5,Setup: 4 open-weight instruction-tuned multilingual LLMs (Gemma-3-4B-it, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, Aya-Expanse-8B). CoT perturbations for mistakes/paraphrases are generated by Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct. Evaluation uses up to 250 questions per subtask, 8 languages, 8 subtasks.

Main findings (the provided full-text excerpt does not include all table values):
- On knowledge-intensive subtasks, CoT faithfulness (reliance) increases as language resource level decreases; on reasoning-intensive subtasks, this trend is weak or absent.
- The direction of the knowledge-vs-reasoning gap is consistent across models and multiple faithfulness metrics and survives robustness checks.
- Confidence partially mediates the resource-level effect, but does not explain why the effect is much stronger for knowledge-heavy tasks.
- Reliance is more frequent in low-resource languages (example reported: ~4% of answers in English vs ~35% in Yoruba show reliance), rather than being uniformly stronger per instance.

Notable methodological note: their “Paraphrasing” intervention is not meaning-preserving in practice; it flips answers about twice as often as Adding Mistakes, which matters for anyone reusing this evaluation protocol.67:T44b,The analysis extends a latent-task view of ICL. When the target task is inside the pretrained task support, the paper studies several instruction structures, including fixed supported instructions, fixed unsupported instructions, mixed instructions, and per-demonstration varying instructions. The key statement is conditional: under a fixed model-induced reference extension, a fixed unsupported instruction contributes a bounded continuation cost, while informative demonstrations keep adding task evidence. With enough demonstrations, task identification can dominate the fixed instruction shift. In contrast, if every demonstration introduces its own off-support instruction shift, the shift cost can accumulate with the number of examples, and convergence depends on whether evidence grows faster than this accumulated cost. For unsupported target tasks, the paper does not assume direct posterior selection of a zero-prior task. It instead studies execution along a trajectory of supported subtasks and bounds final error by accumulated step-level errors through a pathwise Lipschitz argument.68:T53d,The central idea is artifact-grounded agentic reinforcement learning. The authors build Webdev-World, an executable environment that exposes code editing, application execution, browser inspection, and on-demand skills through a shared interface while preserving project state across interactions. Rewards are defined through a weighted checklist derived from task requirements. An independent verifier builds, runs, and inspects the final application. Deterministic checks and visual assessments are aggregated into a terminal reward, gated by successful execution and rendering. To avoid relying only on sparse terminal rewards, the method also estimates intermediate requirement satisfaction from project and execution evidence, and uses changes in these estimates as progress and regression signals for stage-level credit assignment. A second important component is training across context windows. The system combines context compaction with message-tree sample construction: when the interaction history grows too long, the textual context is rewritten compactly while the true project state remains in the environment. The message tree preserves dependencies across rewrites so that different stages of the same long trajectory can still be used for training without repeatedly charging the policy loss for the same generated history.69:T47a,The main evaluations are on Webdev-Bench and Vision2Web. Webdev-Bench is introduced to test capability composition within a single project, including requested changes and preservation of specified existing behavior. On this benchmark, WebCoder-27B reaches a requirement satisfaction score of 72.8, compared with 64.8 for its Qwen3.8-27B base model and 65.1 for Claude-Opus-4.8. It also delivers a running application on all 40 tasks. On Vision2Web, WebCoder-27B achieves an average visual and functional score of 62.4 versus 41.1 for the base model, and is essentially on par with Claude-Opus-4.8 at 62.5. The more interesting takeaway is methodological: the paper argues that a single artifact-grounded reward can supervise heterogeneous frontend activities, that intermediate progress signals improve long-horizon credit assignment, and that context compaction plus message-tree training makes it possible to continue learning when trajectories exceed the model’s context window. If the full paper includes ablations, the most important ones to inspect are the removal of progress signals, verifier gating, and cross-window training support.6a:T49e,The framework is called Harness-Policy Optimization (HPO). A coding agent is modeled as a pair of policy P and harness H, with trajectories scored by an executable verifier. Each round alternates two updates. In the H-step, the current policy is frozen and used to analyze its own failed trajectories, propose harness revisions, and select the best candidate on a search set. The harness can modify prompts, context organization, tool usage flow, feedback format, and stopping rules. In the P-step, the selected harness is frozen and the policy is updated with reinforcement learning under that harness. The updated policy then becomes the proposer for the next H-step. Crucially, the harness search set is also updated using hard tasks identified during the preceding policy-learning phase, so harness search stays aligned with the policy’s current weaknesses. The paper instantiates two versions: Self-HPO, where the evolving policy proposes its own harness candidates, and an external-model variant using Opus 4.8 as a fixed proposer. The main design insight is not merely “optimize both,” but to couple proposer identity and search-set construction to the evolving policy.6b:T410,The base policy is Qwen3.5-35B-A3B and the seed harness is mini-SWE-agent. Experiments are run on three coding benchmarks. The headline aggregate result is an average resolve-rate improvement from 57.8% to 64.8%. On SWE-Bench Verified, the base agent improves from 61.0% to 69.5% under Self-HPO, and to 71.6% when the same HPO loop uses Opus 4.8 as an external harness proposer. The gains transfer to out-of-distribution settings, including SWE-Bench Multilingual and SWE-Bench Pro. One of the most important ablations concerns the search set: dynamically updating it with hard tasks discovered during policy learning works better than reusing a fixed set. Another notable result is that Self-HPO improves both pass@1 and pass@k, which suggests it is not only making existing successful behaviors more reliable, but also expanding the set of successful trajectories the agent can reach. The provided text does not expose all RL details or per-round tables, but the paper states that average resolve rate improves at every optimization step.6c:T409,For a skim, start with the decomposition itself: policy defines the capability boundary, harness determines how that capability is elicited. The introduction and Figures 1–2 are enough to understand why harness-only and policy-only optimization are both incomplete. In the method section, focus on the interface between H-step and P-step: how failures are summarized into harness proposals, how candidate harnesses are selected, how RL is run under a fixed harness, and how the hard-task search set is refreshed. In experiments, prioritize three pieces: Self-HPO versus policy-only and harness-only baselines, cross-combinations of optimized policy and optimized harness, and the pass@k plus OOD results. Those are the sections that tell you whether the method is merely overfitting a scaffold or actually improving the agent loop. A quick read takes 20–30 minutes. A deeper read is only necessary if you want to port the idea beyond coding agents, in which case the definition of the harness search space becomes the key detail.6d:T659,The proposed method, SchemaShield-Lite, is a training-free inference-time robustness layer with three components. First, each tool is rendered into a compact canonical card containing the tool name, description, required fields, and field-level type information. This normalizes presentation and emphasizes the current schema. Second, after the base model emits a tool call, a validator checks the tool name, required fields, and argument structure, returning structured error information rather than a binary valid/invalid flag. Third, if the call is invalid, the model is prompted again with the task context, the canonical tool card, the invalid call, and the validator errors, and asked to repair the output. Under the primary non-oracle protocol, repair is staged. If the base call already names a visible tool, the system performs constrained repair against that tool. If the tool name is missing, it first runs a candidate-set retry over visible tools to resolve a concrete target, then performs tool-constrained repair. The paper highlights three implementation details that materially affect results: the repair prompt must explicitly ask the model to infer all required fields from dialogue context; candidate-set retry is necessary when the tool name is absent; and the repair stage needs a larger token budget, raised to 512 tokens in their setup to avoid truncation on longer examples. An important ablation result is that the main gains come from the combination of structured validator feedback and tool-constrained repair, while the canonical card contributes more to prevention than to repair-stage recovery.6e:T54c,The main experiments use Qwen3.5-9B on a 300-example DICE-BENCH slice and a 200-example BFCL slice under a deployment-oriented non-oracle protocol. SchemaShield-Lite improves DICE-300 accuracy from 0.717 to 0.813 and BFCL-200 accuracy from 0.580 to 0.805. The authors also report no observed repair harms on the originally-correct slice in either benchmark, which is important because repair layers often trade recovery for regressions. To rule out the trivial explanation that a second attempt alone explains the gains, they compare against naive retry: on DICE, naive retry reaches 0.727 versus 0.813 with repair; on BFCL, 0.610 versus 0.795. Another central finding is that missing tool names are the main bottleneck in the non-oracle setting. Adding candidate-set retry raises repaired accuracy from 0.780 to 0.813 on DICE and from 0.725 to 0.805 on BFCL, while reducing unresolved repair targets from 35 to 15 and from 34 to 9. In the final primary runs, only 2 of 22 drift misses on the DICE originally-correct slice and 0 of 45 on BFCL remain validator-invisible, which means most drift-induced failures are still detectable and repairable. A supporting replication on Qwen3.5-35B-A3B improves BFCL from 0.635 to 0.845, suggesting the effect is not specific to one model size, though the strongest non-oracle evidence remains within the Qwen family.6f:T438,This is best read as a systems paper. Start with the problem formulation to see how the authors restrict drift to semantics-preserving changes: description drift, schema drift, and candidate-set drift. Then read the method section with attention to the repair pipeline, especially what the validator exposes, when candidate-set retry is triggered, and how forced-tool repair differs from unconstrained repair. In the experiments, prioritize the main result table plus the sections corresponding to the naive-retry comparison and the component ablation, since those answer the two key questions: whether the gains are just from trying again, and which components actually matter. This is a 20 to 30 minute skim for the main idea. The more interesting follow-up question is not the repair layer itself, but what it says about current tool-calling models: they appear highly sensitive to interface surface form. That points toward training-time directions such as schema augmentation, parameter-renaming robustness, or partially decoupling tool semantics from raw textual interfaces.70:T5de,The method has two main pieces. First is a retrieval-based labeling pipeline. Starting from a corpus of user sessions, the system uses an off-the-shelf memory extractor (mem0 in the paper) to produce atomic candidate facts from each session. Under an infinite-budget construction, all extracted facts are added to a growing candidate memory pool. For each candidate fact, the pipeline scans future user turns. A turn is first judged on whether memory is needed; if memory is queried, retrieval is performed. A fact is labeled useful if it is retrieved in a later interaction and its content is actually incorporated into the model’s answer. An LLM judge is used to verify that the retrieved fact was genuinely used, reducing noise from superficial retrieval hits.

Second is Warm-Start UCB. The offline stage learns a shared utility pattern from historical users, providing a prior or initialization. The online stage adapts to a new user with an upper-confidence-bound algorithm under one-sided delayed feedback. Because feedback is observed only for admitted facts and only after an unknown delay, the problem is cast as an apple-tasting style decision process. UCB handles the exploration–exploitation trade-off by favoring facts with both high predicted value and high uncertainty. Compared with pure offline supervision, this explicitly models partial and delayed feedback. Compared with a cold-start contextual bandit, warm start uses population data to avoid learning each user from scratch.71:T4ba,From the provided full-text excerpt, the evaluation is built on PerMemBench-S and an additional longer-horizon synthetic benchmark, PerMemBench-L. The paper reports comparisons across different memory budgets and interaction lengths, against prior LLM-based gating baselines and an admit-all baseline. The main empirical claim is that Warm-Start UCB consistently outperforms both, suggesting that online personalization matters and that the retrieval-derived labels are useful for learning.

The most informative parts are likely not just the aggregate score tables but three supporting analyses. First, label-validity checks in the appendix test whether retrieval-based labels really correspond to future usefulness. Second, the paper analyzes which utility patterns are shared across users and which are user-specific; that directly motivates the two-stage design. Third, the theory argues that warm start helps leverage offline data while the online UCB component moves toward user-specific behavior. litt","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-10-15T21:10:20Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2610_22650.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇论文系统回答一个训练 在预训练中表现强，实际训练中自然会遇到两个问题：预训练用 Muon 后，SFT 是否也必须用 Muon；已有 AdamW 预训练模型，SFT 换 Muon 是否会吃亏。论文的主要结论是：没有看到明显的额外 optimizer mismatch penalty。最终下游表现大多可以由预训练阶段优化器效应和后训练阶段优化器效应解释，交互项很小。optimizer consistency 的确能降低 update distribution，并在每一步以概率 p 选择 Muon update、以 1-p 选择 AdamW update。p 取 0.0、0.1、0.25、0.5、0.75、0.9、1.0。这样可以连续控制一个阶段更接近 AdamW 还是 Muon，并构造预训练 p_PT 与 SFT p_SFT 的二维网格。为了区分切换成本和边际效应，论文把每个格子的性能分解为全局均值、预训练行效应、SFT 列效应和剩余交互项。如果“匹配优化器”本身有收益，交互残差应该在 p_PT=p_SFT 的对角线上系统性更好；实验中这种模式不明显。继续预训练部分也采用类似思路，并指出早期看到的切换成本在为不同设置调 learning rate 后基本消失。实现上，AdamW 使用 decoupled weight decay 和 gradient clipping；Muon 使用论文 Algorithm 2 中的配置，并采用 0.2 RMS update scaling。作者还利用 AdamW β1=0.9 时的 update RMS 估计，使混合优化器可以使用共同的 base learning rate，从而减少尺度差异带来的混淆。","experiments":"核心实验训练 1.2B Llama-3 update 比例通常提升平均下游表现，与预训练阶段用什么优化器关系不大；预训练和 SFT 优化器匹配带来的交互收益很小；SFT 中优化器一致性可以减少 pretraining-distribution forgetting，但不能解释下游得分优势。论文还在公开 AdamW 预训练的 Llama 和 Qwen 系列模型上做 task-general SFT，规模到 14B，Muon 与 AdamW 至少保持竞争力。继续预训练实验显示，未经 learning rate tuning 时可能出现的切换劣势，在为各设置调学习率后大幅减弱或消失。","reading_guide":"建议精读 Experimental Setup、Cost of Optimizer Mismatch、continued pretraining 相关实验、forgetting analysis，以及公开模型 SFT independent quality of each optimizer in each stage. The key question is not whether Muon or AdamW is better in isolation, but whether optimizer mismatch across pretraining, continued pretraining, and SFT has its own cost.","importance":"The result is directly relevant to the pretrain-posttrain interface. If Muon is attractive for pretraining, practitioners need to know whether SFT must also use Muon. Conversely, for AdamW-pretrained open models, they need to know whether switching to Muon during SFT is risky. The paper finds little evidence for a meaningful extra mismatch penalty. Final downstream performance is mostly explained by the marginal effects of the pretraining optimizer and the post-training optimizer, with small interaction effects. Keeping the optimizer consistent can reduce forgetting of the pretraining distribution during SFT, but that reduction does not reliably translate into better downstream benchmark performance. This supports choosing optimizers stage by stage, based on efficiency and validation behavior, rather than enforcing end-to-end consistency.","technical_approach":"$a","experiments":"$b","reading_guide":"Read the experimental setup, the optimizer-mismatch grid analysis, the continued-pretraining section, the forgetting analysis, and the open-model SFT experiments. Most existing work either recomputes the gradient subspace at certain intervals, or performs post-hoc adaptation to catch up to the moving subspace. In this work, we explicitly identify in practice the slow, stable manner in which LLM gradient subspaces drift across time. th","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"must_read","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-10-16T01:13:12Z","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2610_22794.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作解决的是低秩梯度子空间优化在 LLM 训练中最核心、也最容易被简化处理的问题：梯度主子空间不是静止的，而是在训练过程中持续漂移。GaLore 一类方法把低秩优化建立在“当前梯度主要落在一个低维子空间里”这个观察上，但它们通常用周期性 SVD 重算子空间，默认子空间在一段时间内近似不变；或者像 OSD 那样再套一层非凸优化去追赶变化。作者通过全文实验明确指出，LLM 训练里的梯度子空间在 warm-up 之后往往不是剧烈跳变，而是缓慢、连续地漂移。既然漂移是慢的，问题就不该被建模成一次次冷启动重估，而应被建模成在线跟踪。Ori 要解决的正是：在很低 rank、很紧内存预算下，如何用足够便宜、足够稳定的方式持续跟踪这个漂移中的梯度主方向，并把跟踪精度转化成真实训练收益。","importance":"这篇对预训练研究的价值比较直接。第一，它把低秩优化从“压缩技巧”推进到“训练动态建模”层面，指出性能瓶颈不只是 rank 小，而是 projector stale。第二，它给出一个对大模型预训练更实用的答案：如果梯度子空间是慢漂移的，那么高频、小步、连续更新的 projector 比低频、大步、周期重算更合适。第三，它在 very low-rank regime 下给出明显收益，这一点很关键，因为 rank 足够高时很多方法都能工作，真正难的是 rank 远小于隐藏维度时每一维都很贵。文中在 1B LLaMA 预训练上把相对 full-rank Adam 的性能差距最多缩小 69.3%，而且计算预算仍然处在较低水平，这说明方法不是只在理论上更优，而是在预训练场景里有实际含金量。\n\n贡献类型上，它兼有三层：训练动态观察、优化器设计、以及带漂移条件的理论保证。量级上，它不是改写主流全参训练范式，但对受内存约束的预训练、持续训练、边缘集群训练、以及低秩 optimizer
